A third very important pillar is financing: Medium-sized enterprises need more than others to support financing. To this end, we have the AWS, which upgrades their loans, grants and guarantees for equity accordingly, and we are working very clearly on winning more venture capital, especially for medium-sized and small start-ups.

In the case of Austria, it is mainly IT and highly specialized workers, who are mostly in a job relationship and who have 98 percent additional earnings from this activity. I myself have seen in practice that this is used again and again. So what do we have to do? – First, we need to see that for the digital transformation, the IT know-how, the digital know-how, is increasing accordingly – we have established the Fit4Internet platform to help employees of the companies to increase their digital know-how – and second, there will be digital pro bootcamps; also, in combination, leading companies and medium-sized companies. I would like to refer to Mr Hartinger-Klein on labour law developments. I see a certain challenge on this subject, but it is not as big in Austria as it is in other international markets.

Point Report of the Economic, Industrial and Energy Committee on the Government Template (478, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law on Civil Engineering (Civiltechnikergesetz 2019 – ZTG 2019) (530 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-28-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00068_d8e13437	Ladies and gentlemen, we are now coming to the 2nd place. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Haubner is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-28-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00068_d8e13464	Madam President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen at the gallery! It's about civil engineering law now. With this innovation, we are reforming and modernising the professional right for this professional group today. The areas in which civil engineers have the power to provide services are specific to each civil engineer. The extent of the powers of the individual civil engineer includes the entire spectrum of his individual university or technical education. So who's affected by this novel? – On the one hand, the architects and, on the other, the engineering consultants in the fields of construction, water construction and environmental engineering, measurement, industrial technology, spatial and landscape planning. Civil engineers are entitled to lead the state coat of arms after a successful examination and defense. This also underlines the importance of this profession. The Civil Engineering Act has a very long and very moving history, which we add today with this innovation a chapter of modernisation. This modernisation and reform was necessary because the requirements for the profession have changed dramatically over the last 15 years. Many young, committed personalities strive for this profession; especially in my native country of Salzburg, Mr. Landeshuptmann recently defended nine young civil technicians. What do we innovation with this law? – The main point of view is the merger of the civil engineering law and the civil engineering chamber law into a federal law. This federal law consists of two main pieces: The first regulates professional law and the second regulates professional representation by the Civil Engineering Chamber. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The key elements of this professional law are, on the one hand, the liberalisation of the rules on practical activity – this is very important because they do not allow bureaucracy – and, on the other hand, the issue of digitalisation, in particular, the possibility of making applications electronically, which is also in line with the government's main objective, namely to become more digital in Austria. In addition, those seeking civil engineering authority will be able to be admitted to the Chamber as extraordinary members of the professional representation in the future. I think that this is an important step for those young people who are working in this profession. Our objective of bringing together and liberalising professional and chamber law is achieved by this law, and we are pleased to do so. I want I would like to turn to all those who have made it possible to create a good law here, especially Mr. Anton Bernbacher and his team for profound innovation. The profession of civil technicians thus has a modern, contemporary civil engineering law, and this is important for our location. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-28-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00068_d8e13753	Mrs. Köstinger has now been reported to speak. - Here you go.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-28-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00068_d8e13775	Madam President. High house! Today, in the representation of our Minister for Economic Affairs Margarete Schramböck, I am happy to see the possibility to explain briefly what this bill is about. Generally speaking, the aim of the Civil Engineering Act 2019 is to bring about the merger of the previous law and the Civil Engineering Chamber Act. It also includes a number of liberalisation measures. One of the main points of the draft is the liberalisation of access to work for civil technicians. As you know, admission to examination requires practical periods of up to one year. These are already to be acquired during the Master's degree course or the final phase of the degree course. Furthermore – and that was a special concern to the Federal Government – the periods of maternity protection will also be considered as practical times. In addition, it will provide for the possibility of submitting applications for admission to the civil engineering examination and the granting of authorisation by electronic means. The new law allows civil engineers to be represented in the event of prevention. The purpose of this new regulation is to ensure the continuation of business even in the long absence of the civil engineer. In the field of chambers, the possibility of extraordinary membership is provided for professional candidates. The aim of this scheme is to give future civil technicians the opportunity to participate in the further development of the profession very early on. [[Abg. Loacker: All chambers...!]] Unlike the original draft, the implementation of the Civil Engineering Act remains in the Federal Ministry of Digitalization and Business Location. As before, the Chamber of Civil Engineers should have the opportunity to deliver an opinion on each request and the final decision remains with the Ministry. This concept of close cooperation has proved its worth over the years and is, above all, maintained for this reason. I should like to thank you very much for your attention, and I very much hope that my colleague, Federal Minister Margarete Schramböck, will be very much in agreement with the proposed law. Thank you.

This hard work on these steep surfaces is also time-consuming, is cost-intensive, and you can imagine that you also need special devices for that. This is precisely what we need to do to compensate for this economic disadvantage and, at the same time, to bring a decent income to our rural family farms. Of this income the farmer must live, and if he no longer has this income, then he has an economic problem. Then he will no longer manage his business, and then society will have a big problem. Then what do we have? – The danger of avalanches, erosion protection is lost, our beautiful cultural landscape, which many millions of guests will enjoy with us and ourselves, will no longer be present, and there will be no more biodiversity. That is why we need these funds from the common agricultural policy: to support these rural family farms, but also to further develop our Austria and, of course, Europe in the future. I can only promise that in Brussels we will together do our best to ensure these funds for our family businesses, but also for rural areas. I am also very confident that the Federal Government will support us – with our Federal Chancellor Sebastian Kurz, with our Minister Elisabeth Köstinger – so that we can negotiate a truly strong agricultural budget for our farms and for us in Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-28-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00068_d8e15807	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) High house! I believe that climate change and its consequences and effects have really arrived in Austria at the latest since the last summer, summer 2018. Building on the last years was especially dry the last summer and hot as never before. We had massive harvest losses, including decreases in the water area and massive pests. These are just a few noticeable consequences. The agriculture and forestry sector in particular is heavily affected in Austria. We are rightly talking about the climate crisis as one of the biggest global challenges of our time. One thing is also quite clear to say: Any support in the fight against climate change is correct and important because it helps to increase the attention of this issue and to address the solutions more closely. Yes, I think it is good that young people deal so intensely with this issue that they also respond to their concerns. Fridays for Future makes it visible that climate protection is a movement based on Greta Thunberg, who is now also present all over the world. One thing is clear: The Federal Government takes the pupils of Fridays for Future seriously. Climate protection is important to us. I also had the opportunity to discuss these issues in a personal discussion with some of the initiators. In any case, we share one goal: This is the reduction of CO2 emissions. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] If you, Mrs Cox – I really welcome the fact that we are dealing with this issue this afternoon – but call for concrete measures, I must ask you if you have the opportunity to take part in votes, to assume this responsibility as well. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] We have an up-to-date issue, which I would like to address, namely the eco-stromnovel: Biomass power plants must be removed from the grid due to the expiry of the tariff This is the first time that we have been able We could have created, together with a constitutional majority here in the house, the basis for a transition into a rather simple procedure until the Renewable Development Act came into force. This concerns highly efficient plants, which are now in operation and which must then be removed from the network. You, too, have not taken this responsibility and have voted against this transitional arrangement. Yesterday in the Council of Ministers, we were led by a new law of principle, and we will put it into nine implementing laws, because this is really a concern for us as a federal government. It is not enough simply to demand climate change and to vote against any small measure here in this House. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] The same is done by the SPÖ. It would have been relatively easy to vote in the Federal Council for the interests of the federal states, to vote in favour of the interests of biomass power plants, to vote in favour of the interests of the eco electricity in that country and simply to agree so that we can create a transitional solution. We've started this ourselves now, and within a very short time we've set up a basic biomass law, and we're going to implement it so that we don't lose any investment. That's what it's all about! You cannot take permanent action on the one hand and then, as I said, always oppose it. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] You all know, and I would like to say quite clearly: As the first measure in my Federal Ministry, we have developed the joint integrated climate and energy strategy #mission 2030 – with a broad process of civil participation, with a parliamentary survey here in this House, with scientists, with all parties, in order to really have a starting point for how we can tackle this energy transition, this transformation of the economy in order to achieve our climate and energy goals. It is the long-term conversion of our energy system. We want to convert the power supply to 100 percent for renewable energy by 2030. That's going to be fixed. In particular, the Renewable Extension Act, which will be available until the summer, will provide important impulses so that we can succeed. This is also the key word: Sector coupling, especially Greening the Gas, to bring the fossil fuels out of our cycle and replace them with renewables. That's our goal. My vision is that in the future every house in Austria can be a power plant that produces the energy itself in an integrated energy and economic cycle, 100 percent renewable, and thus can make a real and important contribution to climate protection. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We visited Denmark this week with a delegation from Members of this House, and we looked at how this energy transition is being carried out in Denmark. Denmark is a pioneer in many areas. We have a common goal that we share: 100 percent electricity from renewable energy sources by 2030. We have looked at different projects, especially network development, of course a very crucial issue if we are serious about the energy transition. One thing we all noticed very quickly: The very big difference between Austria and Denmark is that in Denmark there is absolute unity between society, parties, the economy and industry, that this expansion of renewable energy is indispensable, that we must do everything we can to achieve this 100 percent in terms of energy consumption. To create renewables really so that we can get rid of nuclear energy in Europe, so that we can get away from coal stream in particular. That's the difference with Austria, and that's what we're going to take to Austria in particular. There we still have, I think, the biggest chunks to deal with, so that we all have the same goal. Austria now covers more than 70 percent of electricity from renewable sources, without nuclear power. We are ahead of many countries and simply show in all sense that this is very well done. With regard to nuclear energy in Europe, we as the Federal Government have long been fighting for a change of course. There must not be a renaissance of nuclear power development in Europe under the cover of climate protection – and that must be stated clearly. That would be completely the wrong way. As a federal government, we really are against this. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] We have filed complaints against the expansion of the Hinkley-Point-C power plant in Great Britain. We complain of Paks II in Hungary. We want the Euratom Treaty to be revised to make it clear that, above all, we are also making the most of Brexit as an opportunity to revise the Euratom Treaty. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Neubauer]] We were recently in Slovakia, I met my colleagues, especially with regard to the commissioning of the Mochovce nuclear power plant. This too is a very crucial and important point. There are massive concerns about safety standards. These are the really central issues we are dealing with here. Also very important: In order for us to succeed in this active climate protection, so that we can also succeed in the energy transition, the building area plays a really crucial role. We have the goal of saving 3 million tonnes of CO2 equivalent over this area. We are currently also developing a common heat strategy with the federal states, which are largely responsible for it. We are also implementing measures from the climate and energy strategy, which include the exit from fossil energy sources. A concrete measure, Mrs Cox, is the out-of-the-oil bonus. We have now paid more than 25 million euros to replace the 700 000 oil heaters that we still operate in Austria with renewable energy systems. Each Austrian receives 5 000 euros when it comes to a renewable energy system and rises from fossil oil. This is also a concrete measure that we have preferred, which we are already implementing and in which our federal states are really active, good partners. That's where we're already seeing the numbers that something is going to change. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] The biggest area and the biggest challenge we see is mobility. We have seen in the 2016 greenhouse gas balance, which is available to us, that the figures are falling, particularly in the economic and industrial sectors. In the agricultural sector, we also saw in the greenhouse gas balance a minus ahead of the figures in 2017, but especially the mobility sector is a huge topic. In the last year, the Federal Government has also begun to act in concrete terms. We have launched an e-mobility package, which is not just about promoting financial nature, but about which it has been important for us to provide general incentives. We want free parking for e-mobility in Austria to be enabled, we want to open bus tracks – just as Norway did this mobility turn. We have decided an exception for the IG-L that we have now taken e-vehicles on the trail. Together with Federal Minister Hofer, we have launched a mobility package of 93 million euros, with which the changeover to clean mobility really takes place. This involves not only e-mobility, but also hydrogen. So we're also trying to roll new technologies out of the red carpet. Loading infrastructure and the like is also part of it. Because you've been talking about concrete measures: We have used the Council Presidency massively for climate protection. A Europe that protects was the motto of the Federal Government; a Europe that protects climate was the motto of my house. We have negotiated a total of four Council formations and have decided and implemented climate change measures in each area; they have also been confirmed by the European Parliament this week. The most impressive example is the reduction of 37.5% CO2 emissions in cars by 2030. Do you know what that means? – An annual savings of 180 million tonnes of CO2, more than double the total emissions of Austria. This is a result that can really be presented under Austrian Presidency! [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] We have also succeeded in anchoring a massive CO2 reduction not only for the cars, but also for heavy traffic. Trucks, freight transport is one of the main issuers and factors, we see this also in all greenhouse gas balances. The CO2 reduction will have to be 30 percent by 2030. – This too is a success of this Austrian Presidency. In the transport sector, we really have a lot to do. I believe that a solution is a key to public transport. In the course of the #mission 2030, the competent Federal Minister Hofer has also undertaken to save 7.2 million tonnes in the transport sector – we have jointly worked out this. Public transport will be a major part of the solution. The Minister takes in EUR 2.3 billion to invest massively in the ÖBB, to expand networks to make public transport cheaper in the future. The possibility of not having to change to the car, but also of coming from the rural areas to the conurbation centres and returning again should be offered. This is active climate protection. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! As has already been said, agriculture is above all the main victims of climate change. We are now suffering massive damage. Agriculture will also contribute to climate protection. In the next CAP reform, we will also make it clear that there are package of measures that will also help agriculture to contribute to climate protection. The rural family farms in Austria are part of the solution and not part of the problem. Our farms are partly in massive competition with huge international agricultural groups. As a result, the grip on regional, seasonal agricultural products produced in Austria is an absolute contribution to more climate protection, something everyone can do. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] But above all we want to start in the ground area. One of the most important storage media we have on CO2 is soil fertility. We want to start, above all, in the context of the CAP reform, so that on the one hand, we can also support agricultural holdings more strongly, but on the other hand, we also want agriculture to make an active contribution to climate protection. Another important issue is my particular concern: the reduction of plastic. Plastic is an important material, but not if it is used once, especially when it contributes to the pollution of the seas and the environment. The raw material of plastic, of plastic is oil, thus also a massive CO2 source. As the Federal Government, we have a comprehensive package to reduce plastic waste in preparation. The ban on plastic carrier bags was, in fact, for almost decades a requirement that we have now taken into account. This is an equivalent of 7 000 tons of plastic, which can thus be saved. One thing is quite clear: We do not want this to be replaced by a one-way alternative, but the keyword of the future is several-way. Each product must be used several times, but above all it must also be added to a fabric cycle. The completion of the real circular economy in Austria is essential. We must not waste anything more, but everything must simply be used again as a resource, as a raw material. However, the ban on plastic sacks is not enough. We want to ban 25 percent of the packaging plastic, save it and thus bring about a significant CO2 reduction again. The topic of microplastics is also very important to us. We are negotiating at European level. If this does not result in a uniform, rapid decision, we will generally ban microplastics in production in Austria. There is also a very clear goal: This will take place by 2020. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] They have raised a number of points that we are currently dealing with very intensively: Tax reform. In this respect, the Minister of Finance has already announced that, at the first stage of the tax reform, which is a key issue for the discharge of the low-earners, the beneficiary of low-income income in Austria, there will also be ecological components that will present this federal government. We have already enshrined in the climate and energy strategy a lot that will really help us to: Elimination of own electricity tax. For the purpose of producing energy you should not also pay taxes in the future, but it should also be an incentive to move towards a renewable energy system. We want to get a lot on the way here, which actually makes a difference. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] Make a difference on the subject: Last year, we took the R20 summit, the Austrian World Summit, to look at ourselves together with the French, which we can achieve on controls at European level. I am an absolute champion of a CO2 minimum price at European level. I do not think any of national CO2 taxes, because that would be far too small in the steering effect. To have competitiveness in order to make a real difference, we need common European solutions. I would really like this Fridays-for-future movement to bring exactly what we have been trying for a year to anchor a CO2 minimum price at European level, so that there are the same rules for our economic and industrial location, so that there are the same rules for companies, so that, above all, significant CO2 is actually saved throughout Europe. We are moving forward on this issue. We have been able to win the Danes in the last few days to work together at European level. You see, honourable Mrs Cox, the #mission 2030 is already in implementation, is already on the way. We work every day, every week in the Council of Ministers, to implement it continuously. The automatic splitting of pensions could thus be a good contribution to reducing the poverty of old age, especially in women. Currently, the pension split is voluntary, and last year – a record year – we had a total of 412 couples who took advantage of the pension split – at 87,000 births a year, i.e. a negligible number. Alexander Biach, chairman of the main association of social security funds, took part in an event on 7 March. March explains that the pension insurance institution is “intensive” to split – literally. So this comes out when the pension insurance institution operates a little intensively with six million insured persons: Then we have 412 cases in a year. The Minister for Social Affairs has stated in the Committee that she is now making a directory, and this folder will send it to all general doctors and to all the female doctors. I then asked what the target of this folder is and what number of cases of pension splitting it wants to achieve when it sends out the directory. – There is no target. We spend tax money, we spend insurance money – measure without target. What is the problem, and why should the pension division not be as voluntary as now, but automatically? – It is still that women in Austria earn less than men on average. Women also earn a little less if they do not have children, but above all, women earn much less when they have children, and also less than their sexes who do not have children. Women work much more often and much longer in part-time than men. Last year, it was 47 percent of the women employed self-employed who worked part-time; in men, the value was about 11 percent, for comparison. With this high part-time rate among women, the Austrians are second in Europe. In front of us are only the Netherlands, but with the difference that in the Netherlands women work on average 30 hours a week part-time, so full-time employment is much closer than with us. This, of course, has an impact, especially on the pension, because if you work less over a very long period of time and thus logically earn less, then you cannot end up with as much pension as if you had fully worked the same time. And what you can't hide: If career breaks take place and these career breaks are long, then a person in employment, a worker, never again. They do almost a professional break for children, then rise again in part time, stay in part-time for another ten years – they will never reach the same income as anyone who has not had this family break. It is also necessary for men to lose wages when they take a paternity break, but the wage losses of men are not as high as those of women. What measures can be taken to address this difference? – A point that would lead to an improvement in women’s pensions in particular would be a faster approximation of the age of women’s pensions to that of men. A second point would be a uniform distribution of care work between women and men, which in fact means we need an increase in father participation when, for example, it concerns karenz. And a third measure would be the automatic pension division to ensure that women in old age are economically better placed and better protected. It is also necessary to look realistically at the facts: Today, when partnerships are made, this does not necessarily have to be a marriage. People get children together and are not married, relationships are split up, and many things are thought of in divorce, but you probably don't think about the pension account. Therefore, such things should go automatically and not be reserved for persons who think so far that they will retire once in 40 years and then play a role in pension account credits. For this reason, our group has already submitted a request for an automatic pension split with opt-out option in the previous legislative period. This request was rejected at the time. They thought: On this horse we rely on it!, and have overlooked that on 1.1.2017 even the framework conditions for the pension split have changed. It would be nice if the NEOS could be updated every now and then. Now they are a little bit caught because we have delayed it with the reason that we are already working on it and no longer need additional support for our minister. That is why we will not agree to the deadline proposal either. Mr Loacker, the grass does not grow faster, even if you draw it, and not if the NEOS draw it. [[Abg. Wöginger: Except you have a warm rain!]] [[Abg. Loacker: But you should read it!]] The pension split is properly implemented and properly negotiated. All things that need to be considered – because there are not only the classical family, but very many different constellations with patchwork family et cetera – are being properly worked out and then implemented. Why I am so glad to speak about it has the following reason: We note again and again – and this too has been mentioned by Mr Loacker – that a lot of people do not know about the splitting of pensions, which is already available and which one can apply for. I would therefore like to give the information to all eligible men like women: It is already possible to voluntarily transfer up to 50 percent of its partial credit to the parent. Recipients don't have to be the woman, so can the man if he's home or only working half-days and raising the children. This can be done for the first seven years after the birth of a child and applied for until the tenth year of life of the child. That means if you have children and one of them is not yet ten years old, you can apply for this for seven years of life for your child also retroactively. The transfer shall be made from the balance of the person who deserves more, to the pension account of the other person or person who has done the part of the education. Mr Loacker has already said that there are very few people who are currently taking advantage of this. That's why my appeal is really to everyone: Think about it! Go to the homepage of the pension insurance institution, there everything is described in detail! Please use this! I would also like to motivate this because this balance is not retransferable. In the event of separation, divorce, one does not have to give it back, it belongs to one and is really a good way to prevent poverty in old age and in the case of women in particular. The only thing that matters is that you have to get it yourself. You have to make a request to carry out the so-called opt-in. Of course, we also work to ensure that, when the mandatory pension split comes, there is an opt-out option for those couples who do not want it. Let's say it again. Mr Loacker, thank you very much for allowing me to explain this. As I said, we unfortunately do not agree, we are already working on it.

The funds for EU compensation are high, but they are not fairly distributed. We demand that funding from a certain area be palpably flattered. We need maximum limits. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Moreover, criteria such as sustainability and environmental kindness must play a key role in the distribution, as it cannot be supported by large-scale land-owners with support of millions, while small businesses are increasingly under pressure. These small-scale farms, whose farms are passed on from generation to generation, want to maintain respectful handling of animals and the environment and not by chemical means their soils. It is precisely this pressure that forces farmers to move in a direction they do not want at all. A bad harvest can mean it for a small farmer today. The Austrians deserve better: an environmentally friendly and sustainable agriculture that protects the farm animals and, above all, the insects! Ladies and gentlemen, the daily work of our farmers must again be worth something!

ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-28-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00068_d8e20857	Madam President. High house! I should like to thank Mr Strasser and Mr Linder as well as Mrs Doublebauer very much for this request. Austria has benefited greatly from increased EU integration and, above all, from trade expansions. For us, of course, especially in the sense of the Austrian farmers, it is central that we actively participate in trade rules and that we anchor them, above all, in the sense of our high standards. Exports are now essential for balanced agricultural markets and especially for our producer prices in Austria. Since 1990, the volume of Austrian agricultural and food exports has increased from EUR 1 billion to more than EUR 11 billion a year and has therefore also developed a lot better than, for example, the export of industrial goods. What Austrian agriculture is concerned with, however, is quality production and are mainly refined products, which can thus also achieve higher value added. jobs in agriculture and processing are, of course, very central. The issue of quality seals and markings plays a crucial role in nature. The AMA seal and the AMA bio-label are state-certified seals and a success story to which some other Member States of the European Union envy us. If it is clear where the raw material comes from in Austrian food, this naturally also promotes sales in Germany and abroad. The export of quality products is an agricultural economic necessity for Austria. For example, every second litre of Austrian milk is currently exported, 60 percent of the products are mainly used in Germany or Italy. The protected designations of origin are also of particular importance. Austria is very successful with some of its unique features in agricultural exports, examples are the freedom of genetic engineering in agriculture, the high proportion of organic and mountain farming, the clean environment and, above all, our landscapes, which are not only a basis for agricultural exports, but also, above all, a real success factor for tourism in Austria. The constantly increasing export figures clearly reflect the success of the previous strategies in our export initiative for agricultural products and foodstuffs, for example, in facilitating veterinary certificates by the Office for Veterinary Certification. In addition, the Federal Ministry of Sustainability and Tourism will develop a comprehensive external economic concept by autumn 2019, including with the focus on agricultural products and foodstuffs. We are concerned that in the future it is easier for consumers to see where raw materials come from, whether they are in the canteens, i.e. in the case of out-of-house consumption, or especially in the case of processed foods. The Federal Government will also focus on this. But above all I can say a warm thank you to all Austrian farmers, who are really responsible for these export hits, that with their very hard work from Vorarlberg to Burgenland, they ensure that we are supplied with perfect, excellent, high-quality food at home and abroad. Thank you.

On the one hand, I believe the issue of Venezuela has been dealt with for the first time in the Council on External Relations much too late, which was only last year in June, a treatment which it would have deserved a very, very much earlier. Who were the key players in bringing it in? – Italy, Spain and Portugal, and For one thing: These three states together have over 1 million double-citizens in Venezuela. That is to say, it is not an issue that now has only a regional political dimension, but it is a subject that has led to the greatest refugee crisis in our time, because for two years now in Venezuela we have left the country more refugees than refugees leaving Syria, and this has only been talking about a certain delay in Europe. For this very reason, we have also provided 1 million euros from the foreign disaster fund for the reception of Venezuelan refugees in Colombia in my resort last year. That too can only be a drop on the hot stone. As has already been said: There is a need for a political solution. In my opinion, this political solution is also on top of a chicken, as an international dimension here is quite, very difficult to bear, and it is called oil wealth. Venezuela is country number one regarding conventional and unconventional reserves, keyword fracking. Oil sands and shale gas, shale oil are raw materials to which not only the US and Russia are now fighting their conflicts, but of course they have become an object of desire, especially the People's Republic of China. Venezuela has not only been in a huge domestic policy, social policy, more human than tragic path and drama for years now, but Venezuela has now become an international commodity game ball playing in the Opec, the organization for oil exporting countries, which is also discussed accordingly in Vienna. The fight for access to these unconventional reserves of oil and natural gas is something that I believe could aggravate this situation, because we have international interests, where the European Union is one of many actors, but of course China, Russia and the USA play their role accordingly. But all the more it is to leave the focus on the Venezuela drama. We shall continue this motion for a resolution and our commitment accordingly, whether within the European Union or in the next UN General Assembly, in the hope that perhaps by then a political solution has been found in this path. If you allow me, I would also like to take a short look at the Brexit issue and some of the other things that happened before. The President of Parliament of the House of Commons has allowed a third meanful vote for tomorrow evening. We are now facing the scenario path again: Is it going to be a positive vote on the exit agreement tomorrow, as it did on 25 May? We have the deadline until 22 November, which was approved by the European Council. May and so to speak have this soft withdrawal of the British Kingdom. If there is a negative vote tomorrow, the 12. April is striking. That means, it's not the one that we've always angrated over and over again. ==References====External links== April just this cliff edge for a hard Brexit, a negative vote should take place tomorrow. We have prepared ourselves accordingly for both the one and the other scenario. If there is a hard Brexit, our main focus will, of course, be on the Austrian citizens in Great Britain, 25 000 in numbers, and the 12 000 British in Austria. We have put in place the measures, for which the legal framework has been set up, whether at law level or on the regulatory basis. That is to say, we have taken all possible measures in this respect over the last year, not knowing whether or not harder or softer Brexit. The other thing is: If it comes to a hard Brexit, it is not just about the fate of the citizens, it is about peace in Ireland and it is about the budget. Many billions of British payments in the EU budget will be missing after a tough Brexit. That means, of course, that requires a reorganisation of net payers, but we do not yet know what the vote will be tomorrow. That means – the black Peter, whoever he can be played – we do not know until tomorrow evening in which direction this giving, extension until 22. ==References== April, will go, but we are de facto prepared for both scenarios accordingly. On the question of EU citizens: Our citizens will also be able to remain in the United Kingdom when it comes to a hard Brexit. As far as British citizens in Austria are concerned, we have drafted a Brexit collection law with novels of all relevant laws, and a very essential point is the innovation of the local and residential law so that British citizens can continue to live and work in Austria. You don't have to do the language and value courses, as far as integration is concerned, because of that, we have exceptions. The Red and White Red Card plus, which has been prepared for British citizens, covers all the framework conditions accordingly. I should like to say very briefly that the government would not take appropriate care of the essential issue of arms exports et cetera in the European Union's work programme. I should like to point out: We have a very massive debate in the European Union today between Berlin and Paris about German arms embargoes, for example concerning the Arabian Peninsula. We had the debate on the export of military weapons to Saudi Arabia and the United Arab Emirates at the end of October. It is an issue in the European Union, it is even one that leads to different lines between the important partners Paris and Berlin on exports. But what Austria can contribute to, since you are assured that we take due account of our neutrality policy and continue to accept this issue, especially when it comes to open wars like Yemen. The issue of desinformazija, troll factories and cybersecurity is an important issue, I remember the debate with Nikolaus Scherak last time. The responsibility for ensuring security, the security course of the elections, is as always within the framework of the electoral authorities, and there the Ministry of the Interior has the highest sovereignty as regards the pure security of the electoral procedure. As regards the programmes, which have also been prepared under the Austrian Presidency for the extension of cybersecurity in recent months, particularly as regards electoral procedures, implementation is under way. So between the last summer and December, we have brought in all kinds of things within the Presidency. We discussed the desinformazija situation in Bucharest in full last February. But it is a national matter where the European Union can only play a part on a conditional basis, but what we have been able to contribute to work programmes has been done. And where there are legislative measures at EU level: they are implemented. I should like to conclude briefly on the issue of climate change and our commitment to this. The World Economic Forum sees climate change as a security issue since the beginning of the zeros, and we have done so in discussions, as well as in the last World Economic Forum. But that we do not implement the fight against climate change in our daily foreign policy accordingly. It is also another reality to which we must stand – but the question of migration as a result of climate change is, of course, something that we have dealt with accordingly within the framework of our EZA. So in our priority countries – I only had guests this afternoon in the run-up to tomorrow's humanitarian congress – this is a topic where we are trying to allow people to stay in their home regions in the context of the implementation of other forms of agriculture. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-28-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00068_d8e23090	Madam President. High house! I would like to deal with the trade agreements between the EU, Colombia and Peru. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Riemer.]] These agreements were, indeed, too controversial before they were voted in the European Parliament, and that was already seven years ago, if not even one of the most controversial agreements – for good reasons, as I would believe. They have been in force since 2013, so we have the opportunity to judge relatively well what has changed since then. Are they, in fact, still as controversial as they were then? Have these free trade agreements met the expectations or not? We also had an expert hearing on these free trade agreements in the Committee. I would also like to sum up my comments on three points along with the comments made by two experts, and I would like to explain why we strongly and vehemently reject these free trade agreements. The first point: The optimistic expectations regarding higher levels of value added in Colombia and Peru have not met in any way. In Colombia, only exports of palm oil and bananas and in Peru of avocado have increased. But what we were able to see there is that all these crops of palm oil, avocado and the like have come from environmental standards of blatant violations of human rights, from fair working conditions. If I follow the opinion of the experts, two of the experts – and they have carried out this very convincingly – believe that there is no economic urgency in accepting these agreements, not a win-win situation for these two states, nor for the European Union. That's the first point. But it's getting even thicker. The second and most central point for me is that the human rights situation in these two countries has worsened dramatically, both in Colombia and Peru. In Colombia, the number of murders of human rights activists and trade unionists has increased to an unbearable level. In Peru it is the criminalisation of human rights defenders, which is simply not acceptable. The third point: The sustainability title lacks sanctions in these agreements. However, sanctions are essential because of the constant violations of labour law, the environment and human rights standards. Therefore, one can only draw one conclusion – one of the experts drew this conclusion –: This agreement is very much in need of revision. Only then can we consider whether or not to agree to this agreement. That's not the case. The agreement was presented as it was then decided in MB. Again: We therefore reject it outright. I don't wonder that the government groups agree. But the fact that the human rights violations that take place there have not been mounted in any way by you, Mr Graf, may be a little surprised. What surprises me, however, is why the NEOS agree to this free trade agreement. Apparently, the NEOS free trade applies to human rights. I wonder how you can have a human rights policy here in this House if you agree to this free trade agreement. – Thank you very much.

We – and I mean Austria as well – already have excellent relations with Canada from tradition. This strategic partnership agreement should lead to regular dialogue at all levels. It is also planned to hold summits every two to three years – probably one in Montreal in April 2019. This shadow financial index is created by the Tax Justice Network. This is an international organization, very recognized, based in the United Kingdom, which is considered to be a fighter against these international tax havens. It shows how accumulated profits are illegally shifted in financial terms. In this index are, of course, Guernsey, Panama, the British Virgin Islands, Bahrain, Jersey and the Bahamas, but still in front of these countries is Singapore – in the fifth place, in the top ranking of money laundering and the shift of illegal financial flows. How does that work? – The index criticizes the lack of financial control mechanisms. Singapore gives little information to the financial authorities of other countries. Here I come to the criticism of the SPÖ of the agreement: Cooperation in the tax field is minimal, it is not part of the agreement; there is only one additional declaration. That's far too weak for us. There are no sanctions mechanisms. The SPÖ, on the other hand, calls for clear rules against these tax havens. In the European Parliament, this was also strongly criticised, including lack of social standards and environmental standards, in concrete terms: no compliance with the rules of the International Labour Organisation. Singapore is a country with nearly six million inhabitants, 40 percent migrant workers. Migrants are essentially second-class people. I can only say to Mr ÖVP and Mr FPÖ: Don't look away! Whoever doesn't look away is Amnesty International. Amnesty International strongly criticizes missing human rights provisions and the situation regarding respect for human rights in Singapore. Of course, companies benefit from these conditions – social standards and human rights are too short, environmental standards are too short. The SPÖ cannot, of course, agree with this situation in the agreement, and social dumping is not in question for us either in Austria or internationally. Thank you.

Since the above-mentioned supplementary or amendment amendments contain constitutional provisions, I shall first put it in accordance with § 82 para. 2 C 1 of the Rules of Procedure, the presence of the constitutional number of Members required for the vote. Mr Pilz, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, have tabled an additional amendment concerning the insertion of a new paragraph 1 and the resulting renumberings in Article 1. I am looking for a sign of approval for those ladies and gentlemen who are speaking in favour of this. – This is the minority, rejected. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who give their approval to give an appropriate sign. – This is the minority, rejected. We shall now proceed to the vote on this part of the draft law as amended by the Committee's report. I am looking for a sign for those ladies and gentlemen who are speaking for it. – This is the majority, accepted. I would ask those ladies and gentlemen to give a corresponding sign that they have given their consent. – This is the minority, rejected. I am looking for a sign for those ladies and gentlemen who are speaking for it. – This is the majority, accepted. We shall now proceed to the vote on this part of the draft law as amended by the Committee's report. I am looking for a sign for those ladies and gentlemen who are speaking for it. – This is the majority, accepted. Finally, we shall vote on the remaining parts of the draft law, together with the title and receipt of the Committee's report. I am looking for those ladies and gentlemen who are standing in for this to be a corresponding sign. – This is the majority, accepted. We shall now proceed to the third reading. I would ask those ladies and gentlemen who are here at the third reading to give an appropriate sign. – This is the majority, so the draft law is adopted at third reading. We shall now proceed to the vote on the motion for a resolution tabled by Mrs Pilz, colleagues, on the "prohibition of party donations for public enterprises and companies which appear in the contractor's catastrophe". I would ask those ladies and gentlemen who are working for this sign to do so. – This is the minority, not accepted.
